# Artificial Intelligence — syllabus

79 syllabus section(s) for this course in the archive, newest batch first. Text comes from the PDF (OCR for scanned pages; check figures against the source). All programmes' syllabi: [`syllabi/`](../../syllabi/README.md).

## B TECH · Computer Science and Business Systems · AY 2025-26 · Sem 6

- Course as printed: Artificial Intelligence (code 702CO0C032)
- Source: `04_Syllabi_Archive/B TECH/3RD YEAR/CSBS/SEM VI/B Tech, CSBS, Sem VI, A Y, 2025-2026.pdf`, page 5-7

````text
Course: Artificial Intelligence   Module Code: 702CO0C032
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Practical   Tutorial
(Hours   (Hours   (Hours   Credit   Internal Continuous   Term End
per   per   per   Assessment (ICA)   Examinations (TEE)
week)   week)   week)   (Marks- 50)   (Marks- 100)
2   2   0   3   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Prerequisite: Data Structure and Algorithms, Programming for Problem Solving.
Course Objective
To impart knowledge of the fundamental theories, methods and techniques in the field of
Artificial Intelligence and to design and develop AI systems.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, students will be able to
1. Explain agents and environment in AI
2. Apply various heuristic and searching strategies to solve problems in the AI domain
3. Design knowledge base using expert systems and game playing
4. Implement supervised and unsupervised learning approaches to solve problems in the
AI domain
Detailed Syllabus
Unit   Description   Duration
1   Introduction to Artificial Intelligence   04
Definitions of AI, Applications of Artificial Intelligence, Concept
of Modeling, Inference and Learning. Introduction to Machine
learning and Deep learning as a subset of AI. Intelligent agents,
concept of rationality, structure of agents, Environment,
Properties of task environment. Real world Examples of agents
and environments.
2   Solving problems by Searching   07
Problem solving agents, searching for solutions. Uninformed
Search: Breadth   first search, Depth first search, Uniform cost
search Informed Search: Informed search strategies, Greedy Best
First Search, A* search, Hill climbing, problems with hill climbing
such as Local Maxima, Plateau, Ridge, Genetic Algorithm.
Adversarial Search: Introduction to the Domain of a game,
Signature
(Head of the Department )   AY 2025-26
SVKM’s NMIMS
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
optimal decisions in games, minimax algorithm, Alpha-beta
pruning.
3   Knowledge Representation   06
Propositional logic, Theory of first order logic, Inference in First
order logic, Forward & Backward chaining, Resolution.
4   Constraint satisfaction Problem (CSP)   05
Constraint satisfaction problems, Backtracking search for CSPs,
variables and value ordering, propagating information through
constraints, Intelligent backtracking, Local search for CSP. Case
study on CSP.
5   Learning   05
Inductive learning, Types of learning, supervised - decision trees
classification, unsupervised learning – K-means clustering.
6   Expert system   03
Definition, model, characteristics, architecture, development
process, limitations, examples of expert systems.
Total   30
Text Books
1. Stuart Russel and Peter Norvig, Artificial Intelligence: A Modern Approach, 4th edition, 2022,
Pearson.
2. Dan W. Patterson, Introduction to Artificial Intelligence and Expert System, Pearson, 1st
edition, 2015.
Reference Books
1. Elaine Rich, Kevin Knight, Artificial Intelligence, 3rd edition, Tata Mc-Graw Hill, 2015.
2. Patrick H. Winston, Artificial Intelligence, 3rd edition, Pearson,2002.
Laboratory Work
8 to 10 experiments (and a practicum where applicable) based on the syllabus.
Signature
(Head of the Department )   AY 2025-26
SVKM’s NMIMS
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Program: B. Tech (Computer Science and Business   Semester: VI
Systems)
````

## B TECH · Data Science · AY 2025-26 · Sem 6

- Course as printed: Artificial Intelligence (code 702CO0C042)
- Source: `04_Syllabi_Archive/B TECH/3RD YEAR/CSE Data Science (311)/SEM-VI/B Tech CSEDS 311 (VT) Sem VI Syllabus AY 2025-26.pdf`, page 3-5

````text
Program: B. Tech. Computer Science and
Semester: VI
Engineering (Data Science) – 311 (VT)
Course : Artificial Intelligence   Code : 702CO0C042
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Practical   Tutorial   Internal Continuous   Term End
(Hours   (Hours
(Hours per   Credit   Assessment (ICA)   Examinations (TEE)
per   per
week)   week)   week)   (Marks - 50)   (Marks - 100)
2   2   0   3   Scaled to 50 marks   Scaled to 50 marks
Pre-requisite: Object Oriented Programming, Data Structures and Algorithms.
Course Objective
To impart knowledge of the current theories, methods and techniques in the field of Artificial
Intelligence. To create ability to analyze, design and develop Al-systems.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, the student will be able to -
1. Formulate Artificial Intelligent problem and identify agents and environments.
2. Apply informed and uninformed searching strategies.
3. Apply knowledge representation schemes for expert systems and game playing.
4. Implement supervised and unsupervised learning approaches to solve problems in AI
domain
Detailed Syllabus
Unit   Description   Duration
1   Introduction to Artificial Intelligence
Definitions of AI, Broad overview of application areas of AI (like vision,
Natural language processing, Robotics etc.) Concept of Modelling,
Inference and Learning. Introduction to Machine learning and Deep   4
learning as a subset of AI. Intelligent agents, concept of rationality,
structure of agents, Environment, Properties of task environment. Real
world Examples of agents and environments.
2   Solving problems by Searching
Problem solving agents, well define problems and solutions, formulating
problems. What is searching? problem as a state space search, Informed
search and uninformed search.
Uninformed Search
Breadth first search, Depth first search, Uniform cost search
Informed Search   6
Informed search strategies, Greedy Best first Search, A* search, Hill
climbing, problem with hill climbing such as Local Maxima, Plateau, Ridge,
Genetic Algorithm.
Adversarial Search
Introduction to Domain of a game, optimal decisions in games, minimax
algorithm, Alpha-beta pruning.
3   Knowledge Representation
Prepositional Logic Syntax, Semantics Knowledge base and inference.
Predicate logic
Facts, Representation of fact, mapping between facts and representation,   4
properties and issues of knowledge representation system, Instance
relationship, Isa, gt & Lt predicate. Unification algorithm, resolution in
predicate and prepositional logic.
AY 2024-25
SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Rules
Procedural versus Declarative knowledge, forward versus backward
reasoning, forward & backward chaining rule system.
4   Constraint satisfaction Problem (CSP)
Constraint satisfaction problems, Backtracking search for CSPs, variables
and value ordering, propagating information through constraints,   5
Intelligent backtracking, Local search for CSP. Case study: Complete
example on CSP.
5   Learning
Forms of learning, Super-vised learning- decision tree classification,
3
Unsupervised learning- K means clustering.   Reinforcement learning-
passive and active reinforcement learning
6   Expert system
Expert system: Definition, model, characteristic, architecture, development
4
process, limitations, and knowledge representation schemes, Case study:
JESS’s rule based engine with RETE algorithm.
7   Case study
Complete application of AI as Natural language understanding and   4
generation, Robotic Vision and Self driving car.
Total   30
Text Books
1. Stuart Russell and Peter Norvig, Artificial Intelligence: A Modern Approach, 4th Edition,
Pearson Education, 2022.
2. Dan W. Patterson, Introduction to Artificial Intelligence and Expert Systems, 1st Edition, PHI,
2015.
Reference Books
1. Elaine Rich and Kevin Knight, Artificial Intelligence, 3rd Edition, Tata McGraw Hill, 2015.
2. Patrick H. Winston, Artificial Intelligence, 3rd Edition, Addison Wesley, 1993 (Classic).
3. Nils J. Nilsson, Principles of Artificial Intelligence, Narosa Publication, Reprint 2002.
4. Robert J. Schalkoff, Artificial Intelligence: An Engineering Approach, 1st Edition, McGraw
Hill, 1990 (Classic).
5. David W. Rolston, Principles of Artificial Intelligence and Expert System Development,
McGraw Hill, 1988 (Classic).
6. Giarratano and Riley, Expert System Principles and Programming, 3rd Edition, PWS
Publishing Company, 1998 (Classic).
Laboratory Work
8 to 10 experiments (and a practicum where applicable) based on the syllabus
_______________________
Signature
(Program Chairperson)
AY 2024-25
SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Program: B. Tech. Computer Science and Engineering
Semester: VI
(Data Science) – 311 (VT)
````

## M Tech · Artificial Intelligence · batch 2024-2026 · Sem 1

- Course as printed: Artificial Intelligence with ML
- Source: `04_Syllabi_Archive/M Tech/Artificial Intelligence/Batch ( 2024 To 2026 )/Semester - 1.pdf`, page 8-11

````text
Program: M Tech Artificial Intelligence   Semester: I
Course: Artificial Intelligence with ML   Code:
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Practical   Tutorial   Internal Continuous   Term End
(Hours per   (Hours per   (Hours per   Credit   Assessment (ICA)   Examinations (TEE)
week)   week)   week)   (Marks - 50)   (Marks- 100)
3   0   0   3   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite: Basic understanding of machine learning & neural network concepts. Familiarity with
python programming and algorithms
Course Objective
Students to get a comprehensive understanding of machine learning algorithms, techniques, and
applications
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, students will be able to –
1. Understand the fundamental concepts of machine learning, including supervised and
unsupervised learning, and their applications in various domains
2. Implement and apply various machine learning algorithms and techniques
3. Evaluate and compare the performance of machine learning models with various metrics and
techniques
4. Understand the importance of need of dimensionality reduction techniques in ML
Detailed Syllabus
Unit   Description   Duration
Machine Learning: Supervised
Definition and purpose of supervised learning - Different types of supervised
````

## MBA Tech · Computer Engineering · batch 2024-2029 · Sem 4

- Course as printed: Artificial Intelligence (code 702CO0C076)
- Source: `04_Syllabi_Archive/MBA Tech/COMPUTER/BATCH (2024-2029)/Semester 5.pdf`, page 3-5

````text
Program: B Tech (CE, CS, AIDS)   Semester: IV/ V /VI
MBA Tech (Computer Engineering)
Course: Artificial Intelligence   Code: 702CO0C076
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Practical   Tutorial
(Hours   (Hours   (Hours   Credit   Internal Continuous   Term End
per   per   per   Assessment (ICA)   Examinations (TEE)
week)   week)   week)   (Marks- 50)   (Marks- 100)
2   2   0   3   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Prerequisite: Data Structure and Algorithms, Programming for Problem Solving.
Course Objective
To impart knowledge of the fundamental theories, methods and techniques in the field of
Artificial Intelligence and to design and develop AI systems.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, students will be able to
1. Explain agents and environment in AI
2. Apply various heuristic and searching strategies to solve problems in the AI domain
3. Design knowledge base using expert systems and game playing
4. Implement supervised and unsupervised learning approaches to solve problems in the
AI domain
Detailed Syllabus
Unit   Description   Duration
1   Introduction to Artificial Intelligence   04
Definitions of AI, Applications of Artificial Intelligence, Concept
of Modeling, Inference and Learning. Introduction to Machine
learning and Deep learning as a subset of AI. Intelligent agents,
concept of rationality, structure of agents, Environment,
Properties of task environment. Real world Examples of agents
and environments.
2   Solving problems by Searching   07
Problem solving agents, searching for solutions. Uninformed
Search: Breadth   first search, Depth first search, Uniform cost
search Informed Search: Informed search strategies, Greedy Best
First Search, A* search, Hill climbing, problems with hill climbing
such as Local Maxima, Plateau, Ridge, Genetic Algorithm.
Adversarial Search: Introduction to the Domain of a game,
optimal decisions in games, minimax algorithm, Alpha-beta
pruning.
Signature
(Head of the Department )   AY 2026-27
SVKM’s NMIMS
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
3   Knowledge Representation   06
Propositional logic, Theory of first order logic, Inference in First
order logic, Forward & Backward chaining, Resolution.
4   Constraint satisfaction Problem (CSP)   05
Constraint satisfaction problems, Backtracking search for CSPs,
variables and value ordering, propagating information through
constraints, Intelligent backtracking, Local search for CSP. Case
study on CSP.
5   Learning   05
Inductive learning, Types of learning, supervised - decision trees
classification, unsupervised learning – K-means clustering.
6   Expert system   03
Definition, model, characteristics, architecture, development
process, limitations, examples of expert systems.
Total   30
Text Books
1. Stuart Russel and Peter Norvig, Artificial Intelligence: A Modern Approach, 4th edition, 2022,
Pearson.
2. Dan W. Patterson, Introduction to Artificial Intelligence and Expert System, Pearson, 1st
edition, 2015.
Reference Books
1. Elaine Rich, Kevin Knight, Artificial Intelligence, 3rd edition, Tata Mc-Graw Hill, 2015.
2. Patrick H. Winston, Artificial Intelligence, 3rd edition, Pearson,2002.
Laboratory Work
8 to 10 experiments (and a practicum where applicable) based on the syllabus.
Signature
(Head of the Department )   AY 2026-27
SVKM’s NMIMS
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management and Engineering
Electronics & Telecommunication Department
Program: B Tech (EXTC/Computer Science/ CSE(DS) 311 (VT)/B
Semester: V / VI / VIII/X
Tech & MBA Tech (Information Technology/Computer
/XII
Engineering)/BTI (Computer Engineering/ Information
Technology)
````

````text
Program: B. Tech. Computer Science and
Semester: VI
Engineering (Data Science) – 311 (VT)
Course : Artificial Intelligence   Code : 702CO0C042
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Practical   Tutorial   Internal Continuous   Term End
(Hours   (Hours
(Hours per   Credit   Assessment (ICA)   Examinations (TEE)
per   per
week)   week)   week)   (Marks - 50)   (Marks - 100)
2   2   0   3   Scaled to 50 marks   Scaled to 50 marks
Pre-requisite: Object Oriented Programming, Data Structures and Algorithms.
Course Objective
To impart knowledge of the current theories, methods and techniques in the field of Artificial
Intelligence. To create ability to analyze, design and develop Al-systems.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, the student will be able to -
1. Formulate Artificial Intelligent problem and identify agents and environments.
2. Apply informed and uninformed searching strategies.
3. Apply knowledge representation schemes for expert systems and game playing.
4. Implement supervised and unsupervised learning approaches to solve problems in AI
domain
Detailed Syllabus
Unit   Description   Duration
1   Introduction to Artificial Intelligence
Definitions of AI, Broad overview of application areas of AI (like vision,
Natural language processing, Robotics etc.) Concept of Modelling,
Inference and Learning. Introduction to Machine learning and Deep   4
learning as a subset of AI. Intelligent agents, concept of rationality,
structure of agents, Environment, Properties of task environment. Real
world Examples of agents and environments.
2   Solving problems by Searching
Problem solving agents, well define problems and solutions, formulating
problems. What is searching? problem as a state space search, Informed
search and uninformed search.
Uninformed Search
Breadth first search, Depth first search, Uniform cost search
Informed Search   6
Informed search strategies, Greedy Best first Search, A* search, Hill
climbing, problem with hill climbing such as Local Maxima, Plateau, Ridge,
Genetic Algorithm.
Adversarial Search
Introduction to Domain of a game, optimal decisions in games, minimax
algorithm, Alpha-beta pruning.
3   Knowledge Representation
Prepositional Logic Syntax, Semantics Knowledge base and inference.
Predicate logic
Facts, Representation of fact, mapping between facts and representation,   4
properties and issues of knowledge representation system, Instance
relationship, Isa, gt & Lt predicate. Unification algorithm, resolution in
predicate and prepositional logic.
AY 2023-24
SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Rules
Procedural versus Declarative knowledge, forward versus backward
reasoning, forward & backward chaining rule system.
4   Constraint satisfaction Problem (CSP)
Constraint satisfaction problems, Backtracking search for CSPs, variables
and value ordering, propagating information through constraints,   5
Intelligent backtracking, Local search for CSP. Case study: Complete
example on CSP.
5   Learning
Forms of learning, Super-vised learning- decision tree classification,
3
Unsupervised learning- K means clustering.   Reinforcement learning-
passive and active reinforcement learning
6   Expert system
Expert system: Definition, model, characteristic, architecture, development
4
process, limitations, and knowledge representation schemes, Case study:
JESS’s rule based engine with RETE algorithm.
7   Case study
Complete application of AI as Natural language understanding and   4
generation, Robotic Vision and Self driving car.
Total   30
Text Books
1. Stuart Russel and Peter Norvig, Artificial Intelligence: A Modern Approach, 3rd edition,
Pearson Education, 2016.
2. Dan W. Patterson, Introduction to Artificial Intelligence and Expert System, 5th Edition, PHI,
2015.
Reference Books:
1. Elaine Rich, Kevin Knight, Artificial Intelligence, 3rd edition, Tata Mc-Graw Hill, 2015.
2. Patrick H. Winston, Artificial Intelligence, 3rd edition, Addison Wesley, 1993(Classic)
3. Nils J. Nilson, Principles of Artificial Intelligence, Narosa Publication, 1997.
4. Robert J. Schalkolf, Artificial Inteilligence: An Engineering Approach, 1st Edition, Mc-
Graw Hill, 1990 (Classic)
5. David W. Rolston, Principles of Artificial Intelligence and Expert System Development, Mc-
Graw Hill,1988 (Classic)
6. Giarratano and Riley, Expert System Principles and Programming, 3rd edition, PWS
publishing company, 1998 (Classic)
Laboratory Work
8 to 10 experiments (and a practicum where applicable) based on the syllabus
````

- Source: `04_Syllabi_Archive/M Tech/Data Science/Batch ( 2023 To 2025 )/Semester - 3.pdf`, page 5-6

````text
Program: M Tech Data Science (Business Analytics)   Semester: III
Course: Artificial Intelligence   Code: 703DB0C017
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Practical   Tutorial   Internal Continuous   Term End
(Hours   (Hours   (Hours   Credit   Assessment (ICA)   Examinations (TEE)
per week)   per week)   per week)   (Marks -50)   (Marks - 100)
4   0   0   4   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite: Machine Learning and Data Mining
Course Objective
Expand the knowledge gained in Database Management Systems in several directions like Non-
Relational data models, deductive (Intelligent) database systems, Distributed systems, web based
systems and object oriented systems etc.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, students will be able to -
1. Design database using concept of extended entity relationship model
2. Implement functions and procedures using concepts of PL/SQL
3. Implement object oriented concepts in database
4. Compare and contrast different types of advance database management systems
5. Describe database Administration and its management
Detailed Syllabus
Unit   Description   Duration
1.   Introduction to AI, history of AI, course logistics, and roadmap   05
2.   Intelligent agents, uninformed search   05
3.   Heuristic search, greedy search, A* algorithm, stochastic search   05
4.   Adversarial search, game playing   05
5.   Machine Learning 1   06
basic concepts, linear models, K nearest neighbours, over-fitting
6.   Machine Learning 2   06
perceptron’s, neural networks, naive Bayes
Machine Learning 3
7.   Decision trees, ensemble, logistic regression, and unsupervised   06
learning
8.   Constraint satisfaction problems   05
9.   Markov decision processes, reinforcement learning.   05
10.   Logical agents, propositional logic and first order logic   05
11.   AI applications to natural language processing (NLP)   04
12.   AI applications and course review   03
Total   60
Text Books
1. Stuart Russell and Peter Norvig., Artificial Intelligence, A Modern Approach, 3rd Edition,
Pearson Education, 2010.
Signature
(Head of the Department)
````

````text
Course: Artificial Intelligence   Code: 703CO0C008 703CO0C008
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme

Prerequisite: Data Structure and Algorithms, Programming for Problem Solving.
Course Objective
To impart knowledge of the fundamental theories, methods and techniques in the field of Artificial
Intelligence and to design and develop AI systems.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, students will be able to -
1. Explain agents and environment in AI
2. Apply various heuristic and searching strategies to solve problems in the AI domain
3. Design knowledge base using expert systems and game playing
4. Implement supervised and unsupervised learning approaches to solve problems in the AI
domain
Detailed Syllabus
Unit   Description   Duration
1   Introduction to Artificial Intelligence   04
Definitions of AI, Applications of Artificial Intelligence, Concept of
Modeling, Inference and Learning. Introduction to Machine
learning and Deep learning as a subset of AI. Intelligent agents,
concept of rationality, structure of agents, Environment, Properties
of task environment. Real world Examples of agents and
environments.
2   Solving problems by Searching   07
Problem solving agents, searching for solutions. Uninformed
Search: Breadth first search, Depth first search, Uniform cost search
Informed Search: Informed search strategies, Greedy Best First
Search, A* search, Hill climbing, problems with hill climbing such
as Local Maxima, Plateau, Ridge, Genetic Algorithm. Adversarial
Signature
(Prepared by Concerned Faculty/HOD)

SVKM’s NMIMS
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Search: Introduction to the Domain of a game, optimal decisions in
games, minimax algorithm, Alpha-beta pruning.
3   Knowledge Representation   06
Propositional logic, Theory of first order logic, Inference in First
order logic, Forward & Backward chaining, Resolution.
4   Constraint satisfaction Problem (CSP)   05
Constraint satisfaction problems, Backtracking search for CSPs,
variables and value ordering, propagating information through
constraints, Intelligent backtracking, Local search for CSP. Case
study on CSP.
5   Learning   05
Inductive learning, Types of learning, supervised - decision trees
classification, unsupervised learning – K-means clustering.
6   Expert system   03
Definition, model,   characteristics,   architecture, development
process, limitations, examples of expert systems.
Total   30
Text Books
1. Stuart Russel and Peter Norvig, Artificial Intelligence: A Modern Approach, 4th edition, 2021,
Pearson.
2. Dan W. Patterson, Introduction to Artificial Intelligence and Expert System, Pearson, 2015.
Reference Books
1. Elaine Rich, Kevin Knight, Artificial Intelligence, 3rd edition, Tata Mc-Graw Hill, 2015.
2. Patrick H. Winston, Artificial Intelligence, 3rd edition, Pearson,2002.
Laboratory Work
8 to 10 experiments (and a practicum where applicable) based on the syllabus.
````

````text
Pre-requisite: Object Oriented Programming, Data Structures and Algorithms.
Course Objective
To impart knowledge of the current theories, methods and techniques in the field of
Artificial Intelligence. To create ability to analyze, design and develop Al-systems.
Course Outcomes
After completion of the course, the student will be able to -
1. Formulate Artificial Intelligent problem and identify agents and environments.
2. Apply informed and uninformed searching strategies.
3. Apply knowledge representation schemes for expert systems and game playing.
4. Implement supervised and unsupervised learning approaches to solve problems in AI
domain

Unit   Description   Duration
1   Introduction to Artificial Intelligence
Definitions of AI, Broad overview of application areas of AI (like
vision, Natural language processing, Robotics etc.) Concept of
Modelling, Inference and Learning. Introduction to Machine learning   4
and Deep learning as a subset of AI. Intelligent agents, concept of
rationality, structure of agents, Environment, Properties of task
environment. Real world Examples of agents and environments.
2   Solving problems by Searching
Problem solving agents, well define problems and solutions,
formulating problems. What is searching? problem as a state space
search, Informed search and uninformed search.

Breadth first search, Depth first search, Uniform cost search
Informed Search   6
Informed search strategies, Greedy Best first Search, A* search, Hill
climbing, problem with hill climbing such as Local Maxima, Plateau,
Ridge, Genetic Algorithm.
Adversarial Search

minimax algorithm, Alpha-beta pruning.

inference.
Predicate logic

system, Instance relationship, Isa, gt & Lt predicate. Unification
algorithm, resolution in predicate and prepositional logic.
````

````text
Unit   Description   Duration
1   Introduction to Artificial Intelligence   04
Definitions of AI, Applications of Artificial Intelligence, Concept
of Modeling, Inference and Learning. Introduction to Machine
learning and Deep learning as a subset of AI. Intelligent agents,
concept of rationality, structure of agents, Environment,
Properties of task environment. Real world Examples of agents
and environments.
2   Solving problems by Searching   07
Problem solving agents, searching for solutions. Uninformed
Search: Breadth   first search, Depth first search, Uniform cost
search Informed Search: Informed search strategies, Greedy Best
First Search, A* search, Hill climbing, problems with hill climbing
such as Local Maxima, Plateau, Ridge, Genetic Algorithm.
Adversarial Search: Introduction to the Domain of a game,
Signature
(Prepared by Concerned Faculty/HOD)   AY 2024-25
SVKM’s NMIMS
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
optimal decisions in games, minimax algorithm, Alpha-beta
pruning.
3   Knowledge Representation   06
Propositional logic, Theory of first order logic, Inference in First
order logic, Forward & Backward chaining, Resolution.
4   Constraint satisfaction Problem (CSP)   05
Constraint satisfaction problems, Backtracking search for CSPs,
variables and value ordering, propagating information through
constraints, Intelligent backtracking, Local search for CSP. Case
study on CSP.
5   Learning   05
Inductive learning, Types of learning, supervised - decision trees
classification, unsupervised learning – K-means clustering.
6   Expert system   03
Definition, model, characteristics, architecture, development
process, limitations, examples of expert systems.
Total   30
Text Books
1. Stuart Russel and Peter Norvig, Artificial Intelligence: A Modern Approach, 4th edition, 2022,
Pearson.
2. Dan W. Patterson, Introduction to Artificial Intelligence and Expert System, Pearson, 1st
edition, 2015.
Reference Books
1. Elaine Rich, Kevin Knight, Artificial Intelligence, 3rd edition, Tata Mc-Graw Hill, 2015.
2. Patrick H. Winston, Artificial Intelligence, 3rd edition, Pearson,2002.
Laboratory Work
8 to 10 experiments (and a practicum where applicable) based on the syllabus.
Signature
````

````text
image and motion picture processing. It also helps to develop a foundation for further

3. Analyze images processed by various morphological and segmentation techniques
4. Comprehend motion picture processing
````

````text
Pre-requisite: Machine Learning and Data Mining
Objectives: Expand the knowledge gained in Database Management Systems in
several directions like Non-Relational data models, deductive (Intelligent) database
systems, Distributed systems, web based systems and object oriented systems etc.
Course Outcomes:
After completion of the course, students would be able to:
1. Design database using concept of extended entity relationship model.
2. Implement functions and procedures using concepts of PL/SQL
3. Implement object oriented concepts in database.
4. Compare and contrast different types of advance database management systems.
5. Describe database Administration and its management.

Unit   Description   Duration
1.   Introduction to AI, history of AI, course logistics, and roadmap   05
2.   Intelligent agents, uninformed search   05
3.   Heuristic search, greedy search, A* algorithm, stochastic search   05
4.   Adversarial search, game playing   05
5.   Machine Learning 1: basic concepts, linear models, K nearest   06
neighbours, over-fitting
6.   Machine Learning 2: perceptrons, neural networks, naive   06
Bayes
7.   Machine Learning 3: Decision trees, ensemble, logistic   06
regression, and unsupervised learning
8.   Constraint satisfaction problems   05
9.   Markov decision processes, reinforcement learning.   05
10.   Logical agents, propositional logic and first order logic   05
11.   AI applications to natural language processing (NLP)   04
12.   AI applications and course review   03
````

````text
| Introduction to Artificial Intelligence
Definitions of AI, Applications of Artificial Intelligence, Concept of Modeling, Inference and Learning. Introduction to Machine learning and Deep learning as a subset of AI. Intelligent agents, concept of rationality, structure of agents, Environment, Properties of task environment. Real world Examples of agents and environments.
|

04
|
2.
| Solving problems by Searching
Problem solving agents, searching for solutions. Uninformed Search: Breadth   first search, Depth first search, Uniform cost search Informed Search: Informed search strategies, Greedy Best First Search, A* search, Hill climbing, problems with hill climbing such as Local Maxima, Plateau, Ridge, Genetic Algorithm. Adversarial Search: Introduction to the Domain of a game, optimal decisions in games, minimax algorithm, Alpha-beta pruning.
|

07
|
3.
| Knowledge Representation
Propositional logic, Theory of first order logic, Inference in First order logic, Forward & Backward chaining, Resolution.
|
06

|
4.
| Constraint satisfaction Problem (CSP)
Constraint satisfaction problems, Backtracking search for CSPs, variables and value ordering, propagating information through constraints, Intelligent backtracking, Local search for CSP. Case study on CSP.
|

05
|
5.
| Learning
Inductive learning, Types of learning, supervised - decision trees classification, unsupervised learning – K-means clustering.
|
05
|
6.
| Expert system
Definition, model, characteristics, architecture, development process, limitations, examples of expert systems.
|
03
|

| Total
| 30
````

````text
Pre-requisite: Students are required to have the following prerequisites:
• Linear algebra (vectors, matrices, derivatives)
• Basic probability theory
• Python programming
Objectives:
• Understanding of learning agent
• Programming for breadth first and depth first search for visiting all possible solution and
picking up the most optimum solution
• Information gathering from data for decision making
Course Outcomes:
After completion of the course, students would be able to:
 Acquire knowledge of the current theories, methods and techniques in the field of AI
 To understand data structure, search algorithm and predicate logic used in AI system
 To formulate and solve AI problems with the aid of abstraction and modeling using AI concepts
Detailed Syllabus: (per session plan)
Unit   Description   Duration
1.   Introduction to AI, history of AI, course logistics, and roadmap,   06
Intelligent agents: agents and environment, uninformed search
2.   Heuristic search technique: Heuristic technique, problems of   10
heuristic technique with real world, different weak methods such as
generate and test, hill climbing, simple hill climbing and steepest ascent
hill climbing, problem with hill climbing such as local maxima, plateau,
ridge, simulated annealing, best first search, OR graphs, A* algorithm,
agenda driven search, AND-OR Graphs, AO* algorithm.
3.   Knowledge Representation logic and rules: prepositional logic and   08
predicate logic, resolution in predicate logic , Constraint satisfaction
problem.
4.   Game playing: Domain of a game, different ways to improve   08
search strategies, plausible move generate, static evaluation
function, different static evaluation functions, minimax search
procedure, adding alpha-beta cutoff, search efficiency of alpha-beta
procedure, futility cutoff, additional refinements such as waiting for
quiescence, secondary search, using book move, iterative deepening,
advantage of depth first iterative deepening.
````

````text
Program: MBA Tech. (EXTC)   Semester : VIII
Course : Artificial Intelligence   Code : BTET08013
(Departmental Elective – 3)
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Lecture   Practical Tutorial   Internal   Term End

3   2   0   4   Marks Scaled to 50   Marks Scaled to 50
Pre-requisite: Programming Concepts, Data Structures and Algorithms
Objectives:
1. To impart knowledge of the current theories, methods and techniques in the field of
Artificial Intelligence.
2. To create ability to analyse, design and develop AI based systems.
Outcomes:
After completion of the course, students would be able to:
1. Understand the basic concepts of AI.
2. Apply various search strategies to solve artificial intelligence problems.
3. Analyse knowledge representation schemes and game playing strategies in AI domain.
Detailed Syllabus:
Unit   Description   Duration
1   Introduction, Intelligent Agents
What Is AI, The Foundations of Artificial Intelligence, The History of
Artificial Intelligence, Applications of AI, Agents and Environments,   06
Good Behavior: The Concept of Rationality, The Nature of
Environments, The Structure of Agents, How the components of agent
programs work.
2   Searching:
Solving Problems by Searching, Depth-first search, Breadth-first search,
Greedy best-first search A* search, Local Search Algorithms, Hill-
climbing search, Simulated annealing search, Local beam search,   09
Genetic algorithms, AND-OR search trees, Searching with Partial
Observations.
15

Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Electronics & Telecommunication Engineering (2021 – 2022)
3   First Order Logic and Inference in First Order Logic
First order logic, Syntax and semantics for first order logic, Using first
order logic, Knowledge engineering in first order logic in electronic   06
circuits domain
Prepositional versus first order logic, unification and first order
inference, forward chaining, backward chaining and Resolution
4   Knowledge Representation
Categories and Objects, Events, Mental Objects and Modal Logic,
Reasoning System for Categories, Reasoning with default information.
Planning   09
Simple Planning Agent, From Problem solving to planning, Planning in
Situation Calculus, Basic representation of Planning, A Partial order
planning algorithm.
5   Uncertainty Knowledge and reasoning
Acting under uncertainty, basic probability notation, Axioms of
Probability, Bayes Rule and its use, representing knowledge in an   05
Uncertain Domain, Semantic of Belief networks, Inference in belief
Networks, Inference in multiply connected belief networks.
6   Learning and Communication
A general model of Learning Agents, Inductive Learning, Learning
Decision tree, Using Information theory, Learning General Logical
Description.   07
Communication as Actions, Types of communicating agents, Syntactic
Analysis,   Definite clause grammar, Semantic Interpretation,
Ambiguity and Disambiguation.
7   Use Cases:
Fraud Detection, Spam Detection, Chat Bot, AI in healthcare   03
Total   45

1. Stuart Russel and Peter Norvig, “Artificial Intelligence: A Modern Approach”, 4th
Edition, Pearson 2019.
````

````text
Pre-requisite: Students are required to have the following prerequisites:
• Linear algebra (vectors, matrices, derivatives)
• Basic probability theory
• Python programming
Objectives:
• Understanding of learning agent
• Programming for breadth first and depth first search for visiting all possible solution
and picking up the most optimum solution
• Information gathering from data for decision making
Course Outcomes:
After completion of the course, students would be able to:
 Acquire knowledge of the current theories, methods and techniques in the field of AI
 To understand data structure, search algorithm and predicate logic used in AI system
 To formulate and solve AI problems with the aid of abstraction and modeling using AI
concepts
Detailed Syllabus: (per session plan)
Unit   Description   Duration
1.   Introduction to AI, history of AI, course logistics, and roadmap,   06
Intelligent agents: agents and environment, uninformed search
2.   Heuristic search technique: Heuristic technique, problems of   10
heuristic technique with real world, different weak methods such as
generate and test, hill climbing, simple hill climbing and steepest ascent
hill climbing, problem with hill climbing such as local maxima, plateau,
ridge, simulated annealing, best first search, OR graphs, A* algorithm,
agenda driven search, AND-OR Graphs, AO* algorithm.
3.   Knowledge Representation logic and rules: prepositional logic and   08
predicate logic, resolution in predicate logic , Constraint satisfaction
problem.
4. Game playing: Domain of a game, different ways to improve   08
search strategies, plausible move generate, static evaluation
function, different static evaluation functions, minimax search
procedure, adding alpha-beta cutoff, search efficiency of alpha-beta
procedure, futility cutoff, additional refinements such as waiting for
````

````text
Unit Description Duration
````

````text
1. To impart knowledge of the current theories, methods and techniques in the field
of Artificial Intelligence.
2. To create ability to analyse, design and develop Al-systems.
Course Outcomes: After successful completion of this course, student will be able to
1. Recognize Artificial Intelligent problem domains and agents.
2. Apply various searching strategies to solve AI problems.
3. Apply knowledge representation schemes for expert systems and game

playing.
4. Understand learning approaches and Natural Language Processing.

Detailed Syllabus:

Unit | Description Duration

1 Introduction to Artificial Intelligence: 4
Definition, the AI problems, Physical symbol system, Physical
symbol system hypothesis, Intelligent agents: agents and
environment, agent types, desirable properties of knowledge,
different AI techniques.

2 Problems, problem spaces and search: 4
Need of a system to solve a problem, problem as a state space
search, requirement of a formal description of a problem,
production system, control strategy & its requirement, Breadth first

search & Depth first search,

3 Heuristic search technique: 8
Heuristic technique, Problems of heuristic technique with real
world, different weak methods such as Generate and Test, Hill
climbing, simple hill climbing and steepest ascent hill climbing,

problem with hill climbing_such as Local Maxima, Plateau, Ridge,

Simulated Annealing, Best first search, OR graphs, A* algorithm,
agenda driven search, AND-OR Graphs, AO* algorithm.

4 Knowledge Representation: 12
Prepositional Logic: Syntax, Semantics Knowledge base and
inference.

Predicate logic: Facts, Representation of fact, mapping between
facts and representation, properties and issues of knowledge
representation system, Instance relationship, Isa, gt & It predicate.
Unification algorithm, resolution in predicate and prepositional
logic.

Rules: Procedural versus Declarative knowledge, forward versus
backward reasoning, forward & backward chaining rule system.
Weak & Strong Slot & filler structure:

Usefulness of slot & filler structure, Semantic net, Intersection
search, representing non binary predicates by semantic net,
partitioned semantic net, Frames. Conceptual Dependency, Script,
Ontology.

iG Game playing: 4

Domain of a game, different ways to improve search strategies,
plausible move generate, Static evaluation function, different static
evaluation functions, Minimax search procedure, adding Alpha-
Beta cutoff, search efficiency of alpha-beta procedure, futility
cutoff, additional refinements such as waiting for quiescence,
secondary search, using book move, Iterative Deepening,
advantage of depth first iterative deepening

6 Learning: 5
General concept about learning, Super-vised, Unsupervised, Rote
and Reinforced learning, learning by taking advice, learning in the
problems solving, learning by chunking, utilities problem, learning
from examples, explanation based learning, decision tree.

7 Expert system: 4
Expert system: Definition, model, characteristic, architecture,
development process, limitations, knowledge representation
schemes, Blackboard Learning Model, Examples of Expert systems.

8 Natural Language Processing: 4
Natural Language Processing: Introduction, Syntactic Processing,
Semantic analysis and Representation Structure, Discourse and
Pragmatic processing, Statistical Natural Language processing,

Spell checking, Application of Natural Language Processing.

Total 45

Pre-requisite: Software Engineering(BTCO05002), DBMS(BTCO03006), Project
Workshop (BTCO06006), Human Computer Interface (BTCO06013)

Objectives: This course prepares students to develop self learning attitude and
working skills through software project development.
Development of the document preparation skills using standard practices

Outcomes: After successful completion of this course, students will be able to

1. Understand the problem statement in the chosen domain.
2. Analyze the problem in hand by conducting Literature Survey.
3. Design solutions for problem identified.

4. Present solution of problem identified and generate relevant documentation.

Detailed Syllabus:

A group of 3-4 students selects the problem definition for the project work
discussed and finalized with the help of faculty mentor. They are required to
develop a project based on three- tier (Front end, logic development, Database)
architecture.

Evaluation:

Each group is expected to maintain the log book. The log book needs to be
evaluated by the mentor every week as the part of continuous evaluation. Each
group must demonstrate the working project, submit the report and do the ppt
presentation at the end of the semester as the part of semester end exam. The exam
can be taken by two examiners: one internal and one external examiner.

Project Report must contain:

1. Problem Definition

2. Originality of the work/Plagiarism declaration

3. Project description

4. Details of development - Methods / Techniques / Data / Charts / Diagrams
5. Database design

7. Project Code & Snapshots/ Output

8. Future scope

9. References.
````

````text
1. To understand complex control systems.
2. To study the implementation principles of intelligent systems.
````

````text
Outcomes:

After the successful completion of this course the student will be able to
1. Analyse intelligent system using Al techniques.
2. Suggest suitable Al solution in system design.
3. Analyse ANN and Fuzzy system implementation.

Detailed Syllabus:

Unit a ) Duration
Description (Hours)

1. Artificial Intelligence:
Overview, significance and role of Al. Overview of application of
Artificial Intelligence. Scope and History of Al. Typical Al Problems, 08
Al Techniques.

2. Al Components, Languages and Strategies:
Definition and Importance of Knowledge, Components of
Knowledge, Knowledge Based System, Knowledge 08
representation, Organization, Manipulation and Acquisition.
Strategies for Knowledge Acquisition, Knowledge Representation
Languages, Issues in Knowledge Representation. A Network
Representation Language.

3. Automated Reasoning:
The General Problem Solver and Difference Tables. Resolution 05
Theorem Proving. Machine Learning. Perceptron Learning, Back
Propagation Learning, Competitive Learning.

4, Knowledge Based Design Aids:
Inference Process, Backward Chaining, Forward Chaining, Hybrid 05
Chaining. Feature Based Modelling, Feature Recognition, Design
by Features. Application of Feature Based Models.

5, Introduction to Neural Network

20
SVKM's NMIMS
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Mechatronics 2016-17

Fundamental Concepts and Models, Learning Process, Learning

Rules, Single Layer Perceptron Classifier, Multilayer Feedforward 08
Network, Single-Layer Feedback Networks. Hamming Net and
MAXNET, Unsupervised Learning of Clusters, Counter propagation
Network, Feature Mapping, Self-Organizing Feature Maps. Cluster
Discovery Network.

6. Introduction to Fuzzy logic
Introduction, Fuzzy Sets, Fuzzy relations, Operations on Fuzzy
Relations, Membership Functions, Fuzzification and 08
Defuzzification, Logic and Fuzzy System, Fuzzy Arithmetic, The
Extension Principle, Fuzzy Associative Memories.

7. Fuzzy System and Applications Decision making with Fuzzy 03
Information, Fuzzy Classification and Pattern Recognition, Fuzzy
Control System, Fuzzy Optimization. Fuzzy-neural applications
examples.

Total 45 Hours

4. Timothy Ross, Fuzzy Logic with Engineering Application, Wiley, 3rd edition,

Algorithms Synthesis & Application, PHI, 1st edition, 2009.
````

````text
Unit Description Duration
1 Introduction to Artificial Intelligence: “

Definition, the AI problems, physical symbol system, physical symbol

control strategy & its requirement, breadth first search & depth first
search,

3 Heuristic search technique: 8

Heuristic technique, problems of heuristic technique with real world,

different weak methods such as generate and test, hill climbing, simple

hill climbing and steepest ascent hill climbing, problem with hill

climbing such as local maxima, plateau, ridge, simulated annealing,

best first search, OR graphs, A* algorithm, agenda driven search,

versus declarative knowledge, forward versus backward reasoning,

structure, semantic net, intersection search, representing non binary 4
predicates by semantic net, partitioned semantic net, frames.

Conceptual Dependency, Script, Ontology

6 Game playing: 4
Domain of a game, different ways to improve search strategies,
plausible move generate, static evaluation function, different static
evaluation functions, minimax search procedure, adding alpha-beta
cutoff, search efficiency of alpha-beta procedure, futility cutoff,
additional refinements such as waiting for quiescence, secondary
search, using book move, iterative deepening, advantage of depth first
iterative deepening

8 Expert system: 4
General concept about learning, Supervised, Unsupervised, Rote and
Reinforced Learning. Expert System : Definition, model, characteristic, iY

architecture, development process, limitations, knowledge
Representation Schemes, Blackboard Learning Model, Examples of
Expert systems.

9 Soft-computing 6
Neural Network : Basic Concept, Model of Artificial Neuron, Neural
Network Architectures : Perceptron, Adaline, Madaline,
Backpropagation Learning Algorithm. Applications of Neural
Network.
Fuzzy Logic: Crisp versus Fuzzy set, Membership functions, Fuzzy

Fuzzy Logic.
````

````text
Program:   B Tech and MBA Tech (All branches Semester:   VII
other than Computer Engineering)
Course:   Artificial Intelligence (Open Elective I)   Code:
Teaching Scheme   Evaluation Scheme
Practical
Lecture   Tutorial   Internal   Term End
Hours   Continuous   Examinations (TEE)
Hours   Hours   Credit   Assessment (ICA)   (Marks- 100
per
per week   per week   (Marks - 50)   in Question Paper)
week
3   0   0   3   Scaled to 50 marks   Scaled to 50 marks
Pre-requisite: Object Oriented Programming, Data Structures and Algorithms.
Objective:
1. To impart knowledge of the current theories, methods and techniques in the field
of Artificial Intelligence.
2. To create ability to analyze, design and develop Al-systems.
Course Outcomes: After successful completion of this course, student will be able to:
1. Formulate Artificial Intelligent problem and identify agents and environments.
2. Apply informed and uninformed searching strategies.
3. Apply knowledge representation schemes for expert systems and game playing.
4. Implement supervised and unsupervised learning approaches to solve problems
in AI domain
Detailed Syllabus:
Unit   Description   Duration
1.   Introduction to Artificial Intelligence:
Definitions of AI, Broad overview of application areas of AI (like
vision, Natural language processing, Robotics etc.) Concept of
Modelling, Inference and Learning. Introduction to Machine   5
learning and Deep learning as a subset of AI. Intelligent agents,
concept of rationality, structure of agents, Environment, Properties
of task environment. Real world Examples of agents and
environments.
2.   Solving problems by Searching:
Problem solving agents, well define problems and solutions,   10
formulating problems. What is searching? problem as a state space
search, Informed search and uninformed search.
B. Tech and MBA Tech /Sem VII /A.Y 2021-22/ Page- 1
SVKM’s NMIMS
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
Uninformed   Search:   Breadth   first   search, Depth   first
search, Uniform cost search
Informed Search: Informed search strategies, Greedy Best first
Search, A* search, Hill climbing, problem with hill climbing such
as Local Maxima, Plateau, Ridge, Genetic Algorithm.
Adversarial Search:
Introduction to Domain of a game, optimal decisions in games,
minimax algorithm, Alpha-beta pruning.
3.   Knowledge Representation:
Prepositional Logic: Syntax, Semantics Knowledge base and
inference.
Predicate logic: Facts, Representation of fact, mapping between
facts and representation, properties and issues of knowledge   8
representation system, Instance relationship, Isa, gt & Lt predicate.
Unification algorithm, resolution in predicate and prepositional
logic.
Rules: Procedural versus Declarative knowledge, forward versus
backward reasoning, forward & backward chaining rule system.
4.   Constraint satisfaction Problem (CSP):
Constraint satisfaction problems, Backtracking search for CSPs,
variables and value ordering, propagating information through   5
constraints, Intelligent backtracking, Local search for CSP. Case
study: Complete example on CSP.
5.   Learning:
Forms   of   learning,   Super-vised   learning-   decision   tree
classification, Unsupervised learning- K means   clustering.   5
Reinforcement learning- passive and   active reinforcement
learning
6.   Expert system:
Expert system: Definition, model, characteristic, architecture,   4
development process, limitations, and knowledge representation
schemes, Case study: JESS’s rule based engine with RETE algorithm.
7.   Case study:
Complete application of AI as Natural language understanding   8
and generation, Robotic Vision and Self driving car.
Total   45
Text Books:
1. Stuart Russel and Peter Norvig, "Artificial Intelligence: A Modern Approach",
Pearson Education, 3rd edition, 2016.
2. Dan W. Patterson, "Introduction to Artificial Intelligence and Expert System",
PHI, 2015.
````
